Joker's flat bonus also moved 500 → 2,500 as part of the same rescale. ✅
15. **Odds-calibrated rarity rebalance + Hand-Rarity Synergy Bonus + Joker sub-tiers** (§3h, §3m), same session as #14. Owner, immediately after #14 shipped: a real Two Pair (900 pts) scored about the same as a High Card with one lucky silver card — rarity was competing with, not rewarding, actual poker skill. Owner asked for three things together, explicitly requesting the odds be worked out: poker hands should count for more, a rare card *combined with* a made hand should pay off exponentially, and a hand rarer than the card should score higher than the card alone. Computed real 5-card poker probabilities against "hand contains this rarity tier" probabilities (§3m) — found gold is actually *more* common than Two Pair, and Joker is rarer than Three of a Kind but more common than a Straight, the opposite of what the old flat bonuses implied. Recalibrated every tier's flat bonus to match its true rarity's position on the hand-score curve, and replaced the old whole-total multiplier (#14) with a Hand-Rarity Synergy Bonus that multiplies *only* the base hand score — zero payoff for a rare card in a worthless hand, a huge one for a rare card in a strong hand. Also added nested Joker sub-tiers (Common/Rare/Epic/Legendary, escalating multiplier up to ×20) per the owner's explicit ask that "the joker itself can have rarities that also multiply." The test-mode admin panel (§12) was extended with one force-button per Joker sub-tier for previewing them. ✅
16. **Four more refinements, same session as #14/#15** (§3h, §3j, §3n, §10): (a) simplified Joker's sub-tiers back down from the just-added Common/Rare/Epic/Legendary ladder to reuse Bronze/Silver/Gold directly — owner: "i want the joker to just have the same rarities as the normal cards"; (b) replaced the baseScore-only Hand-Rarity Synergy Bonus (#15) with true per-card attribution — the multiplier now applies to the *entire* score, but only when a rare card is genuinely part of the winning combination (a matched pair/trips/quads card, any card in a full-board hand, or part of a Three Straight/Near Miss run), not merely present in the hand; owner supplied a precise worked example (a silver 3♠ as an unrelated Pair kicker vs. the same card as part of Two Pair) that the implementation was verified against directly (§3n); (c) reworked the score-reveal layout so the biggest number lands at the *top* of the breakdown list (existing lines animate sliding down via the FLIP technique) while still revealing smallest-to-largest in time, per "10 points appears, then above it comes 20 points and the 10 moves down"; (d) fixed a real visibility bug where selecting a rare card for discard showed almost no selection indicator beyond the lift, because `.card--rarity-*`'s border/glow was silently winning over `.card--selected`'s at equal CSS specificity — fixed with higher-specificity compound selectors. Also bumped the admin panel's Luck slider ceiling from 60× to 500× per "i would like for the rarity slider to go higher." ✅
17. **Four more asks, same session as #14/#15/#16** (§3o, §3p, §3q): (a) fixed the one remaining gap in multi-rarity scaling — two rare cards landing in a hand with neither part of the winning combo now still scores strictly more than one, via a second, smaller multiplier tier scoped to just the flat rarity total; (b) added an unconditional Card Value Bonus (every final-hand card contributes its rank, always) per "base points for each card in the hand, make it balanced with the current points system" — calibrated to the same scale as existing minor bonuses, which in turn required fixing Pity Points (its unconditional floor had pushed pity out of reach — pity now evaluates with Card Value excluded); (c) added Diamond, a fifth rarity tier above Gold and rarer than Joker itself, with a deliberately "insanely high" ×15 multiplier, per "added rarities higher than gold like diamond at first to start"; (d) rebuilt the score breakdown as RNGDLE-style badge cards — icon, name, category tag, points, a plain-English description, and a highlighted 5-card proof strip per line — from a reference screenshot the owner provided, reusing/extending data the engine already had (contributingIndices, suit/flavor match indices, per-card rarity indices) rather than adding a parallel explanation system. ✅
18. **Four more asks, same session as #14–#17** (§3r): (a) discarding a rare card now pays out half of what keeping it would have earned, so a correct discard of a rare card is rewarded rather than just erasing it; (b) a flat +25%-per-extra-rare-card kicker on top of the already-multiplicative rarity multiplier stack, so 2+ rare cards read as more special than the raw tier-product alone; (c) the score breakdown now collapses to the top badge + half of the second (fading to transparent) with a Show More/Less toggle, applied only once the count-up reveal animation has fully finished; (d) the test-mode admin panel gained a Custom Hand Builder — 5 independent rank/suit/rarity slot pickers and a "Deal This Hand" button — alongside the existing luck slider and random-slot force buttons. ✅
19. **Odds-proportional hand-score rescale** (§3s). Owner: the old table's Pair-to-Royal-Flush gap (200 → 5000, 25x) didn't reflect how much rarer a Royal Flush actually is (~274,000x). Offered a moderate surprisal-linear option, an extreme odds-proportional option, or a custom table; owner picked the extreme option — Royal Flush now scores 54,800,000. Every other constant that had silently been hardcoded to an old `HAND_RANKS` value (Perfect Keep, four achievement thresholds, two personality thresholds, the Risk meter's normalizer) was re-derived from the new table via a new `scoreForHandId()` helper instead of being left to drift; the Luck meter's calibration constants were re-tuned empirically via a one-off Monte Carlo script. Score displays gained thousands-separator formatting since numbers now reach eight figures. ✅
20. **Wild Joker identity bug fix** (§3t), owner bug report with a screenshot. A Joker wild-completing a Pair displayed (and scored Suit Synergy/Card Value/Flavor/the whole extra-bonus registry against) its own meaningless dealt rank/suit instead of what it actually substituted to for hand-category detection — two contradictory identities for one card in the same score. Fixed by threading the already-existing (but under-used) "logical hand" substitution through every pattern-detecting bonus function and the score-breakdown's card-proof strip, consistently. Flagged, but deliberately not fixed in this pass: the wild search only optimizes for poker-category score, so ties (like which rank a Pair-completing Joker picks) still resolve to a fixed iteration order rather than whichever choice would maximize the full run's total — a separate, bigger question for the owner to weigh in on. ✅
21. **Rank-scaled hand scores** (§3u), owner follow-up to #20: "it should actually matter what it pairs with... a joker paired with a jack will be worth more than a joker paired with a 2." Every hand category except High Card and Royal Flush now scores along a small range instead of one flat number, linear in that category's decisive rank (the pair/trip/quad rank, or a run's high card), bounded so a category's ceiling can never cross into the next category's floor. Chose the fuller of two scoping options (also scaling Straight/Flush/Straight Flush by high card, not just the matching-rank hands). This also fixes #20's flagged follow-up for free — the wild Joker search already maximizes category score, so it now naturally prefers pairing with a Jack over a 2 with no changes to the search itself. Caught and fixed a side effect: five "category or better" checks (Perfect Keep, Zen Master, Full Send, Shark, Architect) had compared raw score against a flat threshold as a category proxy, which breaks once scores vary within a category — switched all five to compare by `handStrengthIndex()` instead. ✅
22. **Redeal-mid-animation race fixed** (§3v), owner bug report: clicking a "new random hand" admin button while the opening deal (or a discard reveal) was still mid-flip left the old animation loop and the new one both running concurrently against the same shared `cardEls`/`handRow` state — visibly, cards from the old deal kept flipping alongside the new ones. Fixed with a `dealToken` counter every animation loop (`dealInitialHand`, `lockIn`, `revealDrawnCards`) captures on entry and re-checks after every `await`, bailing the instant a newer deal has superseded it. Verified live: rapid back-to-back redeal interrupts during both the opening deal and a post-discard reveal always settle to exactly 5 cards with no leftover stale elements or a stale score shown. ✅
23. **Three Straight / Four Straight promoted to real hand categories** (§3w), owner bug report: "i just got a three straight and the top said the hand was a high card" — these existed only as extra bonuses, stacked independently of whatever the primary poker category actually was. Computed exact probabilities (13.78%/3.10% via exhaustive enumeration) and, per owner's choice of strict odds-proportional ranking over a simpler "always weaker than a made hand" option, slotted Three Straight between Pair and Two Pair and Four Straight (renamed from Near Miss, "rename near miss to four straight also") between Two Pair and Three of a Kind — both objectively rarer than the hand rank below them. Rank-scaled by the run's own high card like Straight/Flush. The extra-bonus registry versions stayed, re-scoped to only fire when a run hides inside a stronger made hand rather than being the whole story, avoiding double-crediting the same run as two badges. ✅
24. **Flying points animation** (§3x), owner request: "cool animations on the actual cards showing the points come off the cards that gave the points and add into the total." Small glowing sparks now fly from each badge's contributing hand-row cards (already known via `highlightIndices`, §3p) to the score total during the reveal, landing right before the total's own count-up starts, with a brief glow pulse on the departing cards. Deliberately no text on the sparks themselves — showing "+N" on every one of a Flush's 5 cards would misleadingly imply each independently earned the full amount; the badge's own number and the running total already carry that. Runs in parallel with the badge's existing count-up rather than adding a fully sequential step, keeping reveal pacing close to what it was before. ✅
25. **Caption Creator rework** (§6a), owner request citing an outside critique of the original design: RNGDLE's captions work because the words have personality (poetic/dumb/internet-humor/absurd, mixed freely) and don't have to make sense together, not because they narrate the hand accurately. Scoped down from the source material's full pitch (which also included an achievement-gated phrase-unlock/rarity/seasonal collection system) to just the caption rework — the unlock system is deferred as its own future project, same shape as §7's not-yet-built Collection Book. Replaced the old 6-slot narrative-beat structure (each piece pinned to discard count/hand category) with 6 flat, freely-mixable pools (Mood/Action/Object/Descriptor/Outcome/Tag); only Outcome still reacts to the hand (Two Pair+ = a "good" pool, per owner's "loosely tied" call over full decoupling). Caught and fixed a content bug before shipping: three phrases had an internal comma that produced a confusing triple-comma line once joined with a neighbor — fixed, with a permanent regression test added. ✅
26. **Caption Creator, reworked again to a single sentence** (§6c), same session as #25 — owner saw §6a's 3-line freeform format live and asked for the source material's *other* example instead: "i dont want it to be a story like it was, i want it to be a short phrase." Re-confirmed the unlock/progression question was still deferred before changing anything. New structure: 6 slots with fixed grammatical roles (Opening/Action/Object/Connector/Ending/Emoji) space-joined into one sentence instead of 3 comma-joined nonsense lines — e.g. "today i trusted the river like it owed me money 💀." `ending` is still the one hand-reactive slot (same Two-Pair-or-better good/bad split, just renamed from `outcome`). Caught a grammar bug via live spot-checking that no unit test could have caught: several `ending` entries carried a leading "and" left over from §6a's format, which collided with any non-"and" connector ("unless and here we are") — rewrote every entry as a bare clause. ✅
27. **Daily pool rotation + vocabulary expansion** (§6d), owner request: "i would like for it to not be the same words everyday so there is room for creativity," plus explicit content asks ("my whole" phrasing, more "slightly inappropriate" humor). Every dropdown was previously one small, permanently-fixed list. Fixed by reusing the daily hand deal's own mechanism — `dailySubset()` shuffles each slot's (now much larger) master vocabulary with a per-day-per-slot seed and shows 16 at a time, same for everyone on a given day, different the next. Vocabulary grew to 35–37 entries for the big slots (opening/action/object), 18 for ending/good and ending/bad, 26 for emoji; `connector` stayed at 14 (a closed set of function words, not a "more variety" category). A proposed batch of "brainrot" slang was explicitly held back at owner's request pending their own pick from the list, rather than added unilaterally. ✅
28. **Vocabulary from #27 reverted** (§6d), same session: owner's verdict on the new content — "undo the phrases you added, they are kinda too corny for what im going for." Reverted every pool to its exact pre-#27 content (opening/action/object back to 26, ending back to 14, emoji back to 22); kept the rotation *mechanism* itself, which wasn't part of the complaint — `ending` and `connector` now both sit at/under the 16-per-day rotation cap, so they show their full pool unrotated, while opening/action/object/emoji still rotate daily. Brainrot still pending owner's picks. ✅
29. **Added "my whole" (bare, no noun after it) to `object`** (§6d) — owner follow-up after the #28 revert. Deliberately incomplete/absurd rather than a filled-in "my whole paycheck"-style phrase (the exact flavor reverted in #28) — e.g. *"my last two brain cells interrogated my whole like he was right ?!"* `object` is now 27 entries. ✅
30. **Daily Modifiers, first pass of 5** (§4a), owner: "i think we should next work on the daily modifiers." Picked Suit Bonus, Flush Frenzy, One Swap, Fourth Chance, and Locked Card — each exercising a different hook (additive scoring, additive-scoring-that-reads-as-doubling, discard limit down, discard limit up, a specific card that can't be discarded) — deferring Lowball/Reverse Rankings (which invert the whole hand hierarchy) to a later pass. Every day has exactly one active modifier, chosen by a pure function of the calendar date (no persistence), with a genuine "no repeat within any 5-day window" guarantee — an earlier implementation's independent per-block reshuffling looked right but allowed repeats 1-2 days apart across block seams; caught by direct testing and replaced with a correct sliding-window construction. `scoreRun()`'s `modifierBonus` param gained an optional callback form so Flush Frenzy/Suit Bonus don't need to re-run hand evaluation (expensive for a wild Joker) just to compute their bonus. `ev-solver.js` gained `excludedIndices` so Locked Card's card can never appear in the "best possible EV" baseline. ✅
31. **Admin panel modifier preview** (§4b), owner: "make a thing in the admin page to change the modifiers." A 4th admin tool — dropdown of all 5 modifiers plus a reset-to-real option, applied on the next redeal, sticky across other admin actions (matching the Luck slider's own persistence). `getDailyModifier()`'s context-resolution logic was refactored into a shared helper so the new `buildModifierById()` (plain `Math.random()`, an ad-hoc preview, not date-seeded) builds the exact same shape without duplicating logic. ✅
32. **Three/Four Straight classification priority fixed** (§3y), owner bug report: "a three straight should be alot more points like we discussed, it was only scoring 30 points for a j q k." §3w's promotion to real hand categories only ever checked run length in the if-chain's final fallback, so a Pair/Two Pair pattern that happened to coexist with a longer run (the paired rank doubling as one end of it) always classified as the weaker Pair/Two Pair, with the run demoted to a throwaway +30/+60 extra-bonus badge. Reordered the if-chain to match `HAND_RANKS`' actual rarity ordering (Four Straight between Three of a Kind and Two Pair; Three Straight between Two Pair and Pair), verified no new ambiguity is possible in either direction. Caught and fixed a resulting performance regression in the same pass (run-length now computed for every Pair-shaped hand, not just bare High Card candidates) with a new allocation-free `runLengthAndHighCard()` helper — net faster than before the fix, not just back to baseline. ✅
33. **Modifiers rewritten to multiply, not add** (§4c), owner: "i think the modifiers should add multipliers to the total if the modifier that is used is in the winning hand, not just bonus points that dont really contribute to the total score." Suit Bonus's flat +60/card became +20%/card (multiplicative); Flush Frenzy's "add the Flush's own score" became a real ×2 on the whole total. `scoreRun()`'s `modifierBonus` param renamed `modifierMultiplier`, applied on top of the rarity-synergy multiplier, defaulting to 1 (no-op) so it's always safe to apply. Same request also asked for Suit Bonus's description to show the actual suit icon instead of its raw letter code — fixed via the already-existing `suitGlyph()` helper. ✅
34. **Modifier multipliers bumped higher** (§4c), immediate owner follow-up: "i think it should be a higher multiplier than it currently is." Clarified scope (both modifiers, a "big bump") before changing the constants: Suit Bonus's per-card rate went from +20% to +50% (max ×2.0 → ×3.5 for a full 5-card suit match); Flush Frenzy's flat multiplier went from ×2 to ×4. Both are single named constants in `modifiers.js`, so this was a pure numbers change — same multiplier plumbing from #33, no scoring.js/board.js edits needed. ✅
35. **Second pass of Daily Modifiers — Second Look, Clean Slate, High Roller** (§4d), owner: "i think we should add a couple more modifiers. i think one should be you have 2 rounds of discards but the second should be less than the first." Clarified the round caps (3 then 1) and the other two picks (Clean Slate + High Roller, over inventing mechanics for the still-undefined roster names) before building. Second Look is the first modifier that's a genuinely new game-loop mechanic rather than a reuse of an existing hook — a full extra discard/draw round inserted before the normal single-round flow, built as `board.js`-only state (`discardRound`, `startRoundTwo()`) with zero new scoring.js/ev-solver.js plumbing; only round 2 is graded (Decision Rating, meters, achievements, personality), round 1 is a deliberately ungraded mulligan, avoiding the much more expensive joint two-round EV solve a fully "fair" version would need. Clean Slate and High Roller both reused existing hooks outright (discardLimit and the unconditional scoring-multiplier branch, respectively) — High Roller deliberately simpler than its original "×1.5, bonuses disabled" placeholder, per owner's scope pick. Roster grew from 5 to 8 with no rotation-logic changes needed (`modifierForDay()`'s no-repeat window already reads `MODIFIERS.length` dynamically). ✅
36. **Double or Nothing** (§4e), owner: "make one where it reveals 2 cards, and then you can do a double or nothing based on those cards seen, and then it reveals the last 3 cards, im not sure if they should be allowed to discard cards on this one or not, you decide what would be more fun." Owner explicitly delegated the discard-or-not call — landed on an asymmetric split (going for it: zero discards, locked into exactly the dealt 5, real tension; playing it safe: a completely normal round, no twist at all) over a watered-down version that allows discards either way. Second genuinely new game-loop mechanic in a row (after Second Look, #35) — a 2-card peek, then either an instant no-discard lock-in or a handoff to the ordinary flow, built by extracting the opening deal's per-card flip loop into a reusable `flipCardsInRange()` helper shared by both the normal deal and this modifier's two-stage reveal. Pair-or-better keeps the ×2; a bare High Card zeroes the total — the threshold isn't arbitrary, it's exactly the boundary HIGH_CARD's own `score: 0` already treats as a miss. Caught and fixed a real display bug this modifier's ×0 case exposed: `revealScore()`'s shown total is an independent accumulation of exactly the badges rendered, not a re-read of `score.total`, and the Modifier Multiplier badge's `> 0` push condition silently hid a negative (wipe) delta — every prior modifier only ever multiplied up, so this never surfaced before. Fixed with a `!== 0` condition, a distinct "💥 Busted — Nothing" badge, and sign-aware `+`/`-` formatting (previously hardcoded to `+` everywhere a badge value is shown). ✅
37. **Two bug reports, same message** (§7a/rarity admin panel): "i think statistician shows up too often, like i just got a decision rating of 27% and still got it" and "on the admin page i set the luck slider to 500x and i only get bronze cards, rarely silver gold or diamond." Statistician's real bug was its threshold, not its measure — `optimalDiscard >= 35` only required beating roughly the bottom sixth of that day's legal discards (a decent, ordinary pick), nowhere near "found the mathematically optimal discard" the label promises; raised to `OPTIMAL_DISCARD_MAX_BONUS - 5` (195/200, now exported from scoring.js instead of a second hardcoded copy). The luck slider's bug was much bigger: shrinking the raw roll before the rarity lookup made bronze the *only* reachable tier once `luckMultiplier` passed ~14× (its own cumulative boundary, 0.07, dwarfing any shrunk roll beyond that point) — getting WORSE, not better, as luck went up. Fixed by scaling the combined chance of landing any rarity at all (capped at 1) while preserving each tier's relative share, the same proportional-renormalization the Joker sub-tier roll already used. Verified live: 500× luck now turns up bronze, silver, gold, and diamond across 20 sampled redeals. ✅
38. **Site header, real accounts, and friends** (§11a), owner: "build a top header part of the website... logo... a question mark... describes how to play... login button if not logged in or the username... along with the friends tab." A genuine scope fork, confirmed rather than assumed: offered a UI-only placeholder version first, owner chose real accounts. A second question locked down the auth method (email magic link, over password/OAuth) and confirmed no Supabase project exists yet — which is the one piece no amount of code can substitute for; everything client-side (schema, Supabase client wiring, auth.js/friends.js, the header/modal UI) is built and tested up to that boundary, with a clear list of the remaining owner-side setup steps (create the project, run `supabase/schema.sql`, configure the magic-link redirect URL, fill in the real project URL/anon key). Caught and fixed a real layout bug the new header exposed: `body`'s `justify-content: center` on a row-flex container (previously only ever centering one child, `#app`) would have laid the header and `#app` out side by side instead of stacked once the header became a second sibling — caught via a live screenshot, fixed by switching to column-direction flex. ✅
39. **Real deployment + two deployment-only bugs** (§11a follow-up): owner went live at cardle.lol via Vercel (git-connected) with a real Supabase project and Resend as custom SMTP. Two bugs only existed once real infrastructure was involved, both fixed: Vercel auto-detecting `public/` as the deploy root (breaking every `/public/...`/`/src/...` absolute path) fixed with `vercel.json`; the test suite crashing the moment real Supabase credentials replaced the placeholder strings (Node can't `import()` an `https:` URL, only a browser can) fixed by gating `isSupabaseConfigured` on `typeof window !== 'undefined'` in addition to the credential check. Also set up git for the first time this session (the repo already existed on GitHub via manual "Add files via upload" — merged with `--allow-unrelated-histories -X ours` rather than force-pushing, so no existing history was destroyed) and established the ongoing workflow: local changes are tested by the owner on `localhost:8080` first, pushed only on their explicit go-ahead. ✅
40. **Duplicate username-prompt modal** (§11b), owner bug report: "when someone successfully logs in for the first time it prompts them to make a username, and when they chose a username it prompts for it again, and it says it is taken." Root cause: Supabase's `onAuthStateChange` genuinely fires more than once per sign-in, and the username-prompt trigger had no guard against firing twice — fixed with a simple "is a prompt already open for this user" flag, cleared via a new `onClose` hook on the shared modal helper so it resets no matter how the modal was dismissed. Verified with a controlled Playwright reproduction (a fake Supabase client that deliberately double-fires `onAuthStateChange`) rather than just reasoning about it, since the real bug depends on Supabase-internal timing that can't be forced from outside. Owner tested and confirmed fixed on localhost before push. ✅
41. **Session not persisting across tabs + Friends tab shown while logged out** (§11b), owner: "whenever i close the tab and open a new instance of cardle.lol it doesnt keep me signed in... i also want to only show the friends tab once the user is logged in." Root cause of the persistence bug: `auth.js` already exported a `getSession()` helper explicitly built for this — its own comment says so — but `header.js` never actually called it, relying solely on `onAuthStateChange`'s initial firing to learn about a session Supabase had already restored from `localStorage`. That's not Supabase's documented, reliable pattern (their own recommendation is to read `getSession()` explicitly on startup, then subscribe for changes) — fixed by calling `getSession()` on init and seeding `currentSession` from it before the subscription's first callback ever runs. Friends-tab fix was a one-line addition to the same `renderAuthSlot()` that already runs on every session change: `friendsBtn.hidden = !currentSession`. Verified both with a controlled Playwright reproduction — a fake Supabase client whose `onAuthStateChange` deliberately never calls back (simulating exactly the gap the fix closes) confirmed the header still restores the persisted session and reveals Friends; a second run with no session confirmed Friends stays hidden and Log In shows. Not yet owner-tested on localhost.
42. **Per-player random hands, not a shared daily deal** (§9.2), owner testing on multiple accounts: "all the players get the same exact cards. i want every user to have a randomly generated draw of cards." The hand seed used to be `dailySeed(date)` — a hash of the calendar date alone, so every player got dealt identically. That's now `persistence.js`'s new `getOrCreateTodaySeed(date)`: a real random seed (`deck.js`'s `freshSeed()`, moved there from a board.js-local copy so `persistence.js` can share it) generated the first time a given day is seen and stashed in `localStorage`, so a reload mid-game still shows the same hand rather than redealing — same "once per day" invariant, just no longer shared across players. Everything else that's still genuinely meant to be identical for everyone on a given day (the Daily Modifier, the story-caption pools) derives its own seed independently via `hashSeed`/`dailySeed` and was untouched — `dailySeed()` itself is kept (still used by test mode's reproducible seeds and its own tests), just no longer what drives the real deal. Verified with a Playwright reproduction: two separate browser contexts (simulating two players) got two different hands; reloading the same context kept the same hand. Not yet owner-tested on localhost.
43. **Account-backed daily play** (§11c), owner: after playing on multiple accounts in the same browser, found that a result played anonymously "stays with the account" — logging into whichever account happened to be active afterward would show it, and logging into a DIFFERENT account showed the same thing again. Root cause: game state was purely local (`localStorage`, keyed only by date, entirely unaware of who — if anyone — was logged in), so it was never actually "attached" to anything; it just sat in the browser and got shown to whoever was there. Owner also flagged the flip side of §42's per-player-random change: refreshing an anonymous tab shouldn't yield a better draw. Asked whether to fix this locally (cheap, but still browser-only) or build it for real; owner chose real. Built to genuinely match RNGDLE's model, per the owner's own description ("sign up before drawing to save your score... sign up to save future scores, this one won't be saved"): a new `daily_plays` table (`supabase/schema.sql`), one row per signed-in user per calendar day, primary-keyed on `(user_id, play_date)` — that primary key IS the server-enforced "exactly one play per day," independent of anything a browser remembers. `seed` is claimed via INSERT (`daily-play.js`'s `claimTodaySeed`) the moment a logged-in player's hand is first dealt, before a single card's touched — reloading mid-game reads the same claimed row back rather than dealing a new one, and `result` fills in via UPDATE at lock-in (`saveTodayResultForUser`). Column-level grants (`revoke update ... ; grant update (result) ...`) stop a signed-in player from using devtools to `.update({ seed: ... })` their own row directly — RLS alone only restricts which *rows* are touchable, not which *columns*, and that gap would have completely undermined the reason this is server-side at all. Anonymous play is untouched from §42 (still `persistence.js`, still one random hand per day per browser) but is now explicitly never written to any account — matching the owner's literal framing, logging in after an anonymous draw starts a genuine fresh account-backed attempt rather than inheriting it. `board.js`'s init had to become async to resolve session state before it can even pick a seed — `test-mode.js`'s `resolveRunConfig()` no longer resolves a `seed` for normal play at all (only test mode still does, synchronously); board.js's new `beginRealPlay()` does that instead, falling back to local anonymous play (with the sign-up hint) if there's no session OR if the Supabase claim throws, so a backend hiccup can't block the game. New `#anon-hint` element carries both messages verbatim from the owner's description. Verified end-to-end with a Playwright reproduction against a mocked Supabase backed by a real local HTTP server (so state persists across separate browser contexts, unlike an in-page mock): anonymous play shows both hints and never writes a row; a logged-in user's seed/result round-trip through the table with no hints shown; reloading as that same user shows the saved result without re-dealing; a second logged-in user gets their own independent seed. Owner tested on localhost and shipped it (commit `af80035`), and the `daily_plays` section of `supabase/schema.sql` **is applied to the live project** — confirmed by the owner, with `select count(*) from public.daily_plays` returning 4 rows. That row count is the useful part: it means `claimTodaySeed`'s INSERT is genuinely succeeding in production, so signed-in players really are getting account-backed seeds rather than quietly falling through `beginRealPlay`'s catch into anonymous local play — a fallback that by design looks almost identical from the outside, so it could otherwise have been inert without anyone noticing.
44. **"Lock In" read as "keep," not "discard"** — owner: a friend playing thought the cards you click were the ones you KEEP, not the ones you discard, and the plain "Lock In" label gave no hint either way. Fixed by putting the actual count in the button itself — `updateLockInButtonLabel()` (board.js) now reads `Discard {selected.size}/{maxDiscards}`, live-updated on every card toggle (previously only recalculated after a deal/round transition, since the label itself never used to depend on the selection). Second Look's round 1 keeps its own "(Round 1 of 2)" qualifier alongside the count. Verified live: 0/1/2 cards marked shows "Discard 0/5"/"Discard 1/5"/"Discard 2/5", and unmarking a card correctly steps the count back down.
45. **Daily Modifier banner not announced as a daily modifier** — owner: "i want the modifier to be more announced that it is a daily modifier." The pill previously just showed the modifier's own name/description with no framing, easy to read as flavor text rather than a distinct daily mechanic. `renderModifierBanner()` (board.js) now renders a small bold uppercase "DAILY MODIFIER" eyebrow above the name/description (matching the small-caps label style already used for `.admin-panel-title` rather than inventing a new one), with the modifier's own name bolded on the line below; the banner itself grew from a thin pill to a bordered box to carry the extra line. Follow-up same session, owner: "make the daily modifer text bigger" — eyebrow bumped 0.65rem → 0.85rem, body bumped 0.9rem → 1.05rem too at first; owner clarified they only meant the eyebrow, so body went back to 0.9rem. Verified visually via screenshots each time.
46. **5-card hand wrapped 4-then-1 on mobile** — owner: "when i open it on mobile there are 4 cards on top and 1 card on bottom, i would like for it to be 3 on top and 2 on bottom." 5 cards + gaps don't fit typical phone widths, so `flex-wrap` broke the row wherever it happened to run out of space — 4-then-1 by default. Capped `.hand-row`'s width (public/styles.css) to fit exactly 3 cards + 2 gaps below 500px, forcing a 3-then-2 wrap instead; flexbox applies `justify-content` per wrapped line, so both rows stay independently centered. Sized the cap off the *larger* 84px card (the `.card` rule's own existing ≥480px breakpoint) since that's the binding case — the smaller 72px cards below 480px fit 3 with room to spare under the same value, so one constraint covers both. Caught and fixed a real boundary bug of my own while verifying: an initial narrower breakpoint (max-width: 479px) left the 480-499px band still broken (it reverts to 84px cards right at 480px, which don't fit 5 across either, so the unconstrained default wrapped 4-then-1 there too) — widened to 499px so the fix's range and the card-size breakpoint's range actually line up. Verified with real browser layout measurements (card bounding-box rows) across 375px through 900px, not just visually: 3-2 from ~320px up through 499px, clean 5-across from 500px on.
47. **Help modal was stale** — owner: "update the help button... to the changes we made, also describe how there are rarities that give multipliers if you have them in the winning hand." The "How to Play" steps (header.js) hadn't been touched since before this session's Lock In relabel (§44) or the Daily Modifier system existed at all. Added a step naming the Daily Modifier explicitly, updated the discard step to reference the actual `Discard X/Y` button text, and added a new step spelling out the rarity-multiplies-the-whole-hand mechanic (§1/scoring.js) in plain terms — a rare card only multiplies the score if it's genuinely part of the final winning combo, not just sitting in the hand unused, and the rarer the card the bigger the multiplier. Verified by actually opening the modal in a browser and reading the rendered text back.
48. **Joker rarity renamed to Wild** — owner: "rename the joker to a wild, i think it makes more sense." Display-only: `rarity.js`'s `label: 'Joker'` → `'Wild'`, plus every literal user-facing string built from it — the on-card label (card-view.js, e.g. "GOLD WILD"), its aria-label, the admin panel's dropdown option and "🃏 Gold Wild" force-preview buttons, the combined rarity-bonus label scoring.js builds (e.g. "Gold Wild"), the test-mode admin-redeal day label, and the help modal's rarity list (§47). Deliberately did NOT rename the internal `id: 'joker'`/`jokerTier`/`hasWildJoker`/etc. used throughout rarity.js/deck.js/scoring.js/hand-evaluator.js/board.js — that's a much larger refactor for no player-facing benefit, and would silently mismatch any already-saved result (a signed-in player's `daily_plays` row, §11c, or local `localStorage`) that recorded `rarity: 'joker'` before this label existed; CSS class names (`.card--rarity-joker`, `.card--joker-tier-gold`, etc.) stayed internal for the same reason. Two of `rarityOptionsHtml()`'s dropdown/button builders were also changed to derive the word from `RARITIES`' own `joker` entry instead of hardcoding it a second time, so a future rename only touches one place. Updated `scoring.test.js`'s two label assertions that hardcoded the old "Bronze/Gold Joker" strings. Verified live: admin panel button, on-card label, aria-label, and test-mode day label all read "Wild" after forcing a Gold-flavored one.
49. **A Draw button, gating the opening deal** — owner: "i want a draw button before you get the cards to start the game, not right when the page is loaded... it should be like the cards are getting dealt and then once they are all in the normal position is when it does the normal reveal." Previously `dealInitialHand()` ran automatically the instant a hand/seed resolved — cards appeared instantly face-down, held 400ms, then flipped. Now a `#draw-btn` (index.html, hidden by default) sits where the hand row will be; the day label, Daily Modifier banner, and sign-up hint (§11c) are still shown immediately since those aren't part of "the cards," but nothing deals until the button is clicked. New `beginWithDrawButton()` (board.js) wraps every FIRST-deal call site (both `beginRealPlay()` branches, plus test mode's own initial deal) — a redeal from the test-mode admin panel is already its own explicit click, so those still call `startHand()` directly with no added gate. `dealInitialHand()` itself now adds each card with a staggered `.card--deal-in` slide/fade-in animation (styles.css) as it's placed in its slot, and waits for every card to actually finish landing before starting the existing flip-reveal, instead of a flat instant-appear + 400ms hold. Caught and fixed a real bug while verifying: `.draw-btn { display: block }` unintentionally overrode the browser's default `[hidden] { display: none }` (both 0-1-0 specificity — an author stylesheet always beats the user-agent one), so setting `.hidden = true` on click stopped actually hiding the button; a Playwright check showed it visually still there despite the attribute being present. Fixed with an explicit `.draw-btn[hidden] { display: none }` override. Verified end-to-end with Playwright: button shows with an empty hand row on load, hides and cards deal in face-down (not yet flipped) right on click, lock-in only enables once the full reveal finishes, and an admin-panel redeal still runs instantly with no button in the way.
50. **Story block: Submit before Copy, and a redesigned share text** — owner: "i want the bottom poem/story part to have a submit button and then only shows the story made and gets rid of the drop downs. the submit button should replace the copy results button and only show copy results once it is submitted. also redesign what is copied from the copy results, showing what cards were drawn and make the poem in quotations." `renderStoryBlock()` (board.js) now has a `#submit-story-btn` where `#copy-btn` used to sit unconditionally — clicking it hides the 6 slot dropdowns (`#story-builder`) and the Submit button itself, revealing Copy Result in the same spot, so it reads as one button changing purpose rather than two stacking up. Share text redesign (`story/generator.js`): new `buildFinalHandText()` shows the actual final hand, full rank AND suit (e.g. "Final Hand: 2♠ 5♥✨ 9♦ J♣✨ K♠", ✨ marking drawn replacements) — replaces the old suit-only `buildEmojiGrid()` line in `buildShareText`'s output specifically because that grid's whole reason for existing (not spoiling a shared daily puzzle for a friend who hadn't played yet) no longer applies now that hands are randomly dealt per player (§42) — nothing shared left to spoil. `buildEmojiGrid` itself is untouched/still exported for any caller that wants the old spoiler-safe version. The story line itself is now wrapped in literal quotation marks in the copied text. Updated the two `story-generator.test.js` tests that depended on the old shareText shape, and added three new ones (final-hand text is exact, ✨ marking + the "(✨ = drawn)" note, and the quote-wrapping). While verifying this end-to-end with Playwright, the exact same `[hidden]`-vs-explicit-`display` CSS bug from §49 turned up AGAIN on `#story-builder` (`display: grid`) — and prompted an audit of every other element toggled via `.hidden =` in board.js/header.js against the stylesheet, which turned up three more real, currently-live instances: `.meters` and `.personality-badge` (both `display: flex` — empty containers, so the bug was there but visually subtle, just an unexplained ~20px gap before each populated) and `.login-form` (`display: flex` — a real, visible bug: the email input + Send Link button stayed on screen after a successful magic-link send instead of being replaced by the "check your email" status text). All three got the same `.selector[hidden] { display: none; }` override and were individually verified — `.meters` via a computed-style check timed to the exact moment it's attached (still `hidden=true`), `.login-form` via a mocked-Supabase Playwright run confirming the form actually disappears after send. (`.wager-prompt` and `.header-friends-btn`, initially suspected too, turned out fine on inspection — no `display` property on those particular selectors at all, a grep false-positive against a similarly-named neighboring rule.)
51. **Drop the ✨ drawn-card markers from the copied text** — owner, same-session follow-up: "remove the stars in the copied." `buildFinalHandText()` (§50) no longer takes a `discardIndices` param or marks replacement cards at all — just the plain final hand (e.g. "Final Hand: 2♠ 5♥ 9♦ J♣ K♠" regardless of what was drawn), and `buildShareText`'s conditional "(✨ = drawn)" note is gone with it. `buildEmojiGrid` (the older, still-exported suit-only grid) keeps its own ✨ convention untouched — this was specifically about the newer final-hand line. Updated the one test that asserted the old star-marking behavior; verified live that a hand with actual discards copies with no ✨ anywhere in the text.
52. **Poem out of the copy, site URL in** — owner, same-session follow-up: "i think we should remove the poem from the copy and just add the website below the final hand." `buildShareText()` (`story/generator.js`) dropped its `storyText` param entirely and now ends with a plain `cardle.lol` line right after "Final Hand: ..." instead of a blank line + the quoted poem. The poem itself is untouched everywhere else — still generated, still shown and editable on-screen via the dropdowns + Submit flow (§50) — it's specifically no longer part of what Copy Result puts on the clipboard. Updated the tests that depended on the poem being in shareText; verified live that the copied text now ends in "cardle.lol" with no quotation marks anywhere.
53. **Site link wasn't clickable when pasted** — owner: "when i paste it the cardle.lol cannot be clicked." `copyToClipboard` (board.js) is a plain `writeText` — most receiving apps' auto-linkifiers (iMessage, Slack, Discord, email...) only reliably turn a bare domain into a tappable link when it has an explicit scheme. `SITE_URL` (§52) changed from `'cardle.lol'` to `'https://cardle.lol'`. Verified live via clipboard read-back.
54. **Whole-project bug sweep — ten fixes** (owner: "scan through the entire project and look for any potential bugs or anything that can be made more efficient"). Every finding below was reproduced before being fixed and re-verified after, in a real browser where the symptom was browser-only. Grouped by what they actually are:

    **Availability (the two that could take the game down entirely).**
    - `supabase-client.js` initialized its client with a **top-level `await import('https://esm.sh/...')`**. Module evaluation is all-or-nothing, so any failure to reach that third-party CDN — outage, corporate/school firewall, ad blocker, offline — left the module un-evaluated and took every importer down with it: auth.js → header.js → main.js and daily-play.js → board.js. Confirmed by aborting all esm.sh requests in Chromium: permanent "Loading today's hand…", no header, no help modal, no Draw button, even though the daily hand, scoring, and help text need no network at all. Now lazy (`getSupabase()`, imported on first real use, promise cached, cleared on failure so a transient blip can retry), and `getSession()` swallows a load failure as "nobody is logged in" — the path anonymous play already handles. `requireSupabase()` became async; every call site in auth.js/friends.js/daily-play.js awaits it, and `onAuthStateChange` stays synchronous for callers by attaching its real subscription once the client resolves. Re-verified with the CDN still blocked: full playthrough works, score renders, help modal opens.
    - Unguarded `JSON.parse`/`localStorage` in `persistence.js` and `stats.js`. `getTodayResult` is called from board.js's init *outside* its try/catch, so one malformed byte — or storage being disabled/full at all (Safari private mode, "block all cookies", quota) — stuck the page on "Loading today's hand…" with no recovery short of clearing site data. Both files now route every storage touch through guarded read/write helpers that treat unreadable storage as "nothing stored yet"; `getOrCreateTodaySeed` additionally rejects a non-numeric stored seed, which used to become `NaN` and silently coerce to seed 0 inside `createRng` (a valid-looking but wrong hand). Verified all four failure modes — corrupt result JSON, corrupt stats JSON, non-numeric seed, and `localStorage` throwing outright — now degrade to a playable game.

    **The EV solver froze the UI for seconds, on ~22% of days.** `solveOptimalDiscard` is exhaustive and ran synchronously inside `lockIn()`. Measured on a machine faster than most phones: 3 discards 222ms, 4 discards (Fourth Chance) 1.2s, 5 discards (Clean Slate) 3.0s — and with a Wild in hand, 4.2s / 14.2s / **15.4s**. All of that was a genuinely frozen page. Three changes, in the order they were tried:
    - **Monte Carlo sampling was tried first and rejected on measurement.** 10,000 samples per option gave up to **78% relative EV error** and moved the Optimal Discard bonus by 71 points out of 200. The reason is structural and specific to this game: HAND_RANKS is deliberately odds-proportional (score ∝ 1/probability, §3s), which by construction makes every hand category contribute roughly equally to the expected value — so the EV depends on rare-but-enormous categories, and one extra sampled quad swings the estimate wildly. Recorded in ev-solver.js so it isn't re-attempted.
    - **Kept the math exact, removed the allocations.** `combinationIndices` yielded a fresh `.slice()` per combination (up to 1.5M throwaway arrays per solve) and `evForDiscard` rebuilt the candidate hand each iteration; both now reuse one buffer, with the single caller that outlives the loop copying explicitly.
    - **Pruned the wild search, provably without changing results.** `evaluateFixedHand` only ever reads suit via `isFlush`, so unless every other card already shares one suit a flush is impossible and the wild's suit cannot affect the category or score — 13 substitutions instead of 52. Behavior-preserving rather than approximate: the search keeps a candidate only on a strictly greater score, so when suit is irrelevant the first suit tried already won every tie. Pinned by a test that brute-forces all 52 and asserts equality.
    - **Moved the solve off the main thread** (`src/core/solve-worker.js` + `src/state/solver.js`, in state/ because it needs the browser `Worker` API that core/ is kept free of). Falls back to solving inline whenever a worker is unavailable or errors — i.e. worst case is the old behavior. Net result: worst case 15.4s → 223ms of *work*, and the worst main-thread stall during the heaviest possible solve measured at **11ms** (verified the worker is genuinely fetched and used, not silently falling back). board.js shows a "Crunching the odds…" state that can now actually animate.

    **Scoring correctness (four bugs).**
    - **A "Busted — Nothing" run paid 270 points.** `pityBonus` wasn't clamped for negative input, and scoreRun legitimately passes one (`multipliedTotal - cardValue.total`, negative when Double or Nothing's bust zeroes the total while Card Value stays 50-350) — so the formula ran past its own documented maximum: `pityBonus(-350)` returned 360. Input clamped at 0, making PITY_MAX_BONUS an actual maximum again.
    - **Flush Frenzy paid nothing on a Straight Flush or Royal Flush.** The check was `id === 'FLUSH'` exactly, on a day whose own text reads "Flushes score 4x today" — landing the rarest hand in poker was worth less than an ordinary flush. Now matches all three flush categories.
    - **Suit Bonus counted a Wild by its meaningless dealt suit** — the exact bug §3t fixed for Suit Synergy, reintroduced through a different door: scoreRun invoked the modifier callback with the *raw* hand, before `logicalFinalHand` existed. A Wild dealt as 2♥ but playing as a spade to complete a spade flush scored as a heart on a hearts-bonus day. The callback now receives the logical hand (and its JSDoc says so).
    - **A second Wild in one hand wasn't wild.** `evaluateHand` used `findIndex`, so only the first was ever substituted; any second kept its junk dealt rank/suit. Two Wilds plus 9♠ J♠ K♠ evaluated as a Three Straight (613) when the real best is a Straight Flush (6,080,000) — now found correctly (10♠ + Q♠). Exhaustive for one or two Wilds (the only counts reachable in real play); three or more, only possible via the admin hand builder, resolve greedily one at a time rather than hanging on a 52ⁿ search. `wildSubstitutions` exposes them all by position; the old single-`wildSubstitution` field still works for every existing caller.

    **Security — stored XSS through usernames.** `schema.sql`'s username CHECK was **length-only**, so the `^[A-Za-z0-9_]{3,20}$` pattern existed *only* in client JavaScript. Since Supabase exposes these tables straight to the browser and the anon key is public by design, anyone could call the REST API directly and store `<svg onload=...>` — 14 characters, comfortably inside the limit — and `header.js` rendered other people's usernames into the friends list via `innerHTML`, executing it in the viewer's browser with their session. Confirmed executing in Chromium before the fix. Fixed on both sides, deliberately: a Postgres regex constraint keeps the value out of the database (`supabase/migrations/001-username-pattern-constraint.sql`, idempotent, with a pre-flight query for existing violations, since the table is already deployed), and `header.js`/`modal.js` escape on output so even a value that somehow gets in renders as inert text. Re-verified with a malicious username served from a mocked backend: no execution, no injected element, name displayed as literal text. **Migration confirmed applied to the live project by the owner** — both halves of this fix are in force.

    **Two dead features.** `meters.js` normalized Risk's "chase" component against ROYAL_FLUSH (~55 million) — a leftover from before HAND_RANKS was rescaled odds-proportionally, when the top of the table was ~5,000. At realistic scores that term contributed **under one point** of its 40 (Two Pair: 0.001), so Risk was just `discardedCount/maxDiscards × 60`, capped at 60 — which in turn made **"The Gambler"** (needs `risk >= 65`) reachable only by being dealt a Royal Flush and throwing it away: 1 of 12 personalities nobody would ever see. Re-anchored to THREE_OF_A_KIND, so abandoning a real made hand now reads as a genuine gamble and The Gambler is selectable in ordinary play (verified end to end through `classifyPersonality`). The meters test's own fixture comment had been written *around* this bug ("Full House alone rounds away to nothing") and is updated.

    **One regression of my own, from §49's Draw button.** `.card--deal-in` is declared `animation-fill-mode: both`, and a filled animation's `transform` outranks any class rule's in the CSS cascade — so the class pinned each card at the animation's final `translateY(0) scale(1)` and silently overrode `.card--flip-out`'s `rotateY(90deg)`. The opening reveal became a hard cut with no flip at all, while the discard-replacement flip still worked, which is why it wasn't obvious. Caught by sampling the computed transform mid-flip (the identity matrix, not a rotation). Fixed by dropping the class on `animationend`; re-sampled after the fix and the transform is now a real progressing 3D rotation.

    Test suite grew 307 → 317, all passing. Its runtime dropped from ~3.8s to ~1.3s as a side effect of the solver work, then rose back to ~3.6s — but only because of a newly *added* worst-case guard (the 5-discard Clean Slate solve, 2.3s on its own) that deliberately exercises the heaviest thing the game can ask for; the pre-existing tests are the ones that got faster.
55. **Poem editing behind an "Edit Poem" button, Copy Result always present** — owner: "i want to always have a copy result button on the bottom. i would also like to rework the poem system so that it gives a random starting poem like it always has done but instead of having all the options immediately, have an 'edit poem' button that then drops down the selections and a submit button after." This partly *reverses* §50: there, Submit occupied the Copy Result slot and Copy Result only appeared once you'd submitted. The problem with that arrangement was that the poem is already complete the moment it renders (it's a seeded random selection — §6c), so gating Copy behind Submit made the player confirm something that was never unfinished. Now:
    - Default state is the finished poem plus one bottom row: `[✏️ Edit Poem] [📋 Copy Result]`. The six slot pickers are collapsed, so an ordinary player never sees a wall of dropdowns.
    - Edit Poem reveals the pickers with a Submit button after them, and hides itself. Submit only *closes* the editor — every dropdown change already applies to the poem live, so there's nothing to commit; it exists to get the pickers back out of the way. Reopening works repeatedly.
    - Copy Result is always visible AND always the bottom-most element. The editor is deliberately placed above the button row in the DOM rather than below it, so opening the pickers can't push Copy Result up into the middle of the block — verified by asserting its measured `getBoundingClientRect().bottom` exceeds both the editor's and Submit's while editing, rather than just eyeballing it.
    - `.story-editor` deliberately declares no `display` property, with a comment saying why: a `block`/`flex` value there would override the browser's default `[hidden] { display: none }` and stop the editor collapsing at all — the same trap that had already hit `.draw-btn`, `.story-builder`, `.meters`, `.personality-badge` and `.login-form` (§50/§54).
    Verified with 22 browser assertions across both render paths (the animated reveal and the "already played today" reload, which share `renderStoryBlock`) plus a 375px mobile pass confirming no horizontal overflow in either state.

---

## 10. Art Direction — cards

Flat, geometric, high-contrast, low-ornamentation:
- Custom SVG suit glyphs, 1–2px stroke weight, no gloss/gradient/drop-shadow "3D render" look.
- Two-tone rank/suit coloring per theme accent (§5) on the card *back* only — faces stay neutral/high-contrast so themes never hurt readability.
- Generous whitespace, clean pip grid layout — closer to a modern Swiss-design deck or Balatro's flat sprite style than a photorealistic casino deck.
- Same visual language extends to the emoji share grid and UI chrome (buttons, modals) for a coherent, non-generic feel.
- **No sound** (§9.6) — feedback is entirely visual/motion: a cascading card-flip reveals replacement cards, and the score builds up line-by-line, lowest to highest (§3j), rather than appearing instantly. Confetti-for-rare-hands from GAME_PLAN.md is still open (not yet built) — though the rare-card glow pulse (§3h) now covers a chunk of that same "make it feel special" goal.
- **Rare cards break the "flat, no gloss" rule on purpose** (§3h) — a colored border/glow scaling with rarity tier, and a slower flip + pulse animation on reveal. Deliberate exception: the whole point is that these read as unmistakably different from the otherwise-uniform deck.

---

## 11. Technical Architecture

```
/core
  deck.js            — seeded RNG, deck construction/shuffle (daily seed only — no arbitrary/unlimited seeding); also rolls each card's rarity (§3h)
  rarity.js           — RARITIES tier list: chance + bonus points + emoji/label, single source of truth (§3h)
  hand-evaluator.js   — pure function: 5 cards -> {rank, score}; wild-Joker substitution search when a 'joker'-rarity card is present (§3h)
  ev-solver.js        — optimal-discard EV computation (Decision Rating + Optimal Discard bonus)
  scoring.js           — base + flavor + suit synergy + skill + extra-bonus-registry + rarity (flat bonus + hand-synergy multiplier, §3m) + pity + modifier bonuses
  bonus-registry.js    — the RNGDLE-style stacking bonus list (§3f)
  meters.js            — Luck / Skill / Risk (§3g)
  personality.js       — run -> archetype classifier (§7a)
  achievements.js      — achievement registry (§7b)
/modifiers
  registry.js          — modifier objects w/ lifecycle hooks: onDeal, onScoreTable, onScore, onDisplay
  (one file per modifier)
/themes
  registry.js          — theme -> {palette, compatible modifiers, story vocabulary}
/story
  templates.js          — FRAGMENTS (structural + flavor phrase pools) + SLOT_META (§6)
  generator.js           — assembles fragments into story text, builds the emoji grid + share text
  vocabulary.js           — rank/suit flavor words
/state
  daily-seed.js        — date -> deterministic seed
  persistence.js        — localStorage: today's full result (score, meters, personality, unlocked achievements)
  stats.js               — localStorage: cumulative history (games played, hands/personalities seen, unlocked achievements) — feeds §7b's cumulative achievements
  test-mode.js            — ?test query param handling; never touches persistence.js or stats.js
  (no cross-day story-preference store — removed per owner request, §6)
/backend (Supabase)
  - anonymous auth on first visit (upgradeable to named profile for leaderboard display)
  - tables: daily_runs(user_id, date, score, decision_rating, modifier_id, theme_id), profiles(user_id, display_name, streak, freeze_tokens, xp), achievements(user_id, achievement_id, unlocked_at)
  - RLS: a user may only insert their own row for *today's* date — prevents replay/backdating
  - leaderboard = simple ranked query per day; skill rating = rolling aggregate over daily_runs
/ui
  cards, animations, board, modals
```

Streamlined vs. v1: no unlimited-mode seed generator, no separate practice-XP track — everything funnels through the one daily run.

### 11a. Site Header, Accounts, and Friends ✅ built (owner: "build a top header part of the website, one that has the logo 'cardle' that you can click to go back to the main page, a question mark clickable icon that when you click it, describes how to play the game simply... on the top right should be the login button if not logged in or the username of the user logged in, along with the friends tab")

**Scope confirmed up front — this was a real fork, not a cosmetic ask.** The project had zero backend before this (§11's original architecture sketch always planned for Supabase, but Implementation Status had it filed under "not yet built"). Asked whether "login" should mean a working header layout with placeholder auth (build now, wire up real accounts as a separate future project) or the real thing. Owner chose the real thing — actual Supabase auth and a real friends system, not a UI mockup. A second question nailed down the auth *method* specifically (email magic link, over password or OAuth — "no passwords to manage... simplest to build and to use") and confirmed no Supabase project exists yet, which shaped how this had to be delivered: the client-side code is fully built and testable now, but real end-to-end login needs the owner to create a Supabase project and hand back its URL/anon key — an external account-creation step no amount of code can substitute for.

**`supabase/schema.sql` (new)** — the two tables this needs, meant to be pasted once into the Supabase SQL Editor: `profiles` (id ↔ `auth.users.id`, a unique `username` Supabase's own auth has no concept of) and `friendships` (directional `requester_id`/`addressee_id` + a `pending`/`accepted` status, rather than two reciprocal rows per friendship). Both tables have Row Level Security enabled with explicit policies — since the anon key is exposed client-side by design, the *database* has to be what actually stops one user reading or editing another's data, not app code alone. Usernames are publicly readable (needed for friend-request lookup and display); everything else is scoped to whoever's actually part of the row.

**`src/state/supabase-client.js` (new)** — the project's first real dependency, deliberately still a plain ESM import from a CDN (`esm.sh/@supabase/supabase-js@2`) rather than an npm package, so "zero build step" stays true. `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` are placeholder strings the owner fills in with their own project's values; `isSupabaseConfigured` gates everything downstream, and the client is only actually constructed (dynamic import + `createClient`) once real values are present — constructing it against the placeholders would throw and break the entire page, including the parts (the daily hand, the help modal) that have nothing to do with accounts.

**`src/state/auth.js`/`friends.js` (new)** — `signInWithMagicLink(email)`, `signOut()`, `getSession()`, `onAuthStateChange(callback)`, `getProfile`/`createProfile` (the one-time username step every brand-new sign-in needs, since Supabase auth itself has no username field); `sendFriendRequest(userId, username)`, `getPendingRequests`, `getFriends`, `acceptFriendRequest`, `removeFriendship`. Every function funnels through one shared `requireSupabase()` guard (`supabase-client.js`) that throws a single clear `SupabaseNotConfiguredError` instead of a confusing null-reference crash when called before setup is finished — this is also what `npm test` exercises, since there's obviously no live Supabase project in CI/this environment (`tests/auth.test.js`/`friends.test.js`, 9 new tests, all offline). `friends.js` deliberately avoids Supabase's embedded-join query syntax (which needs Postgres's auto-generated foreign-key constraint names, an implementation detail this module shouldn't have to hardcode) in favor of fetching friendship rows and profiles separately and merging them client-side — simpler and more robust than depending on exact constraint-naming behavior.

**`src/ui/modal.js` (new)** — a small generic overlay (backdrop click / Escape key / × button to close) shared by every popup this feature needed: the help modal, login modal, username prompt, account modal, and friends panel. Each caller supplies a `render(bodyEl, close)` callback rather than a static HTML string, since several of these need live form handling, not just markup.

**`src/ui/header.js` (new)**, wired into `main.js` alongside (not inside) `initBoard()` — site-wide chrome, independent of board.js's own per-run header (day label, modifier banner). Logo click reloads to `/` (the only page that exists yet, but written to hold up once others do); the "?" opens a 4-step how-to-play modal, no backend involved. The right side reactively swaps between a "Log In" button and a "👤 username" button via `onAuthStateChange` — including the brief gap between a successful magic-link sign-in and that user's profile finishing its fetch (shown as "…" rather than a blank button). Clicking Friends while logged out opens the login modal instead, with a contextual hint ("Log in to see your friends") — the panel itself is only meaningful with a signed-in user id to query against. The friends panel re-fetches and re-renders itself in place after every action (send/accept/decline/remove) rather than hand-patching the DOM incrementally, which is simple and plenty fast for what's a short, infrequently-changing list.

**A real layout bug caught before shipping.** `body` was `display: flex; justify-content: center` to horizontally center `#app` (the only child it ever had before this). Adding `#site-header` as a new sibling meant that same row-flex would have laid the header and `#app` out *side by side* instead of stacked — caught via a live screenshot showing the header rendered as a narrow box instead of a full-width bar. Fixed by moving to `flex-direction: column; align-items: center` (swapping which axis centering happens on) and moving `body`'s old padding onto `#app` specifically, so `#site-header` could span edge-to-edge above it. A second follow-up screenshot round caught that the header still wasn't stretching full-width even after that (missing an explicit `width: 100%` — `align-items: center` alone sizes flex children to content, not the container), fixed the same way.

Verified live via Playwright (everything that doesn't require a real Supabase project): header layout renders correctly in both light and dark mode (screenshot-checked after the flex bug fix above); the help modal opens with its 4 steps and closes via both the × button and Escape; the login modal correctly shows "Accounts aren't set up on this deployment yet" pre-configuration rather than silently failing; clicking Friends while logged out routes to the login modal with the friends-specific hint; the game board underneath is completely unaffected (day label, hand dealing, lock-in all still work). Zero console errors. 303/303 tests passing (16 new: `isValidUsername`'s validation rules, plus every auth.js/friends.js function's not-configured error path).

**Now actually deployed** — the owner set up the real Supabase project, connected `cardle.lol` via Vercel (git-based, auto-deploys on push to `main`), and configured Resend as a custom SMTP provider (`noreply@cardle.lol`) once Supabase's own default email sender's rate limit turned out too low for real use. This surfaced two real bugs outside `src/` itself, both fixed:

- **Vercel was deploying only `public/` as the site root** (its default "Other" framework behavior whenever a `public/` folder exists), so every absolute `/public/...`/`/src/...` path in `index.html` 404'd — the whole page loaded unstyled and stuck on "Loading." Fixed with `vercel.json` (`outputDirectory: "."` + a `/` → `/public/index.html` rewrite), making the deployed layout match what `tools/dev-server.js` already does locally, with zero changes to the project's actual file structure.
- **The test suite broke the moment real credentials replaced the placeholder strings** in `supabase-client.js` — `import('https://esm.sh/...')` only works in a browser; Node's ESM loader rejects a remote `https:` specifier outright, and `isSupabaseConfigured` used to depend only on the URL/key values, so filling those in made every test importing `auth.js`/`friends.js` crash at import time. Fixed by also gating `isSupabaseConfigured` on `typeof window !== 'undefined'`, permanently keeping Supabase out of reach under Node regardless of what's filled in — the same "browser-only, tests skip it structurally" precedent `persistence.js`/`stats.js` already set for `localStorage`.

### 11b. Duplicate Username-Prompt Modal ✅ fixed (owner bug report: "when someone successfully logs in for the first time it prompts them to make a username, and when they chose a username it prompts for it again, and it says it is taken")

**Root cause**: Supabase's `onAuthStateChange` fires more than once for a single sign-in (once restoring `INITIAL_SESSION`, again for the actual `SIGNED_IN` event) — `header.js`'s `refreshProfile()` ran on every firing and, seeing no `profiles` row yet on *either* firing (the first `createProfile()` call hadn't necessarily resolved before the second firing checked), opened a second username-prompt modal stacked on top of the first. Submitting through the stale second modal — after the first had already created the profile — hit `profiles.username`'s unique constraint: "taken" by the user's own, just-created account.

**The fix**: a `usernamePromptOpenForUserId` guard in `header.js`, set the instant a prompt opens and checked before opening another; `modal.js` gained an `onClose` callback (fires no matter which of the four ways a modal closes — × button, backdrop click, Escape, or the render callback's own `close()`) so the guard reliably clears even if the user dismisses the prompt without submitting.

**Verified with a controlled reproduction**, since this depends on real Supabase timing that can't be forced from the outside normally: intercepted the `esm.sh` Supabase SDK import in Playwright with a fake client whose `onAuthStateChange` fires twice (mirroring the real quirk) and whose `insert()` simulates a real unique-violation on a repeat username. Before the fix this would have opened 2 modals; after, only 1 opens despite the double firing, and submitting through it succeeds cleanly (1 insert call, no "taken" error, modal closes, header shows the new username). Zero console errors. No unit-test changes (this is DOM/timing-only, same precedent as the rest of the animation/UI layer). Owner confirmed fixed on localhost before this was pushed.

### 11d. Profile Page, XP & Levels ✅ built (owner: "make a full profile page, once you click on the username top right it takes you to your profile with a whole bunch of stats, your 5 recent hands (with a load more to show more) your highest hand, total points, and this is where you can sign out or delete account at the bottom... i also want a full xp and level system")

Pass 1 of a two-pass build (see decision log #56 for why it was split; pass 2 is the cosmetics layer — badges, titles, name paints).

**Everything is DERIVED from `daily_plays`, with no new stats table.** Every row already stores the complete run result (`score`, `finalHand`, `personalityId`, `decisionRating`, `dayNumber`), so hands played, total points, best score, highest hand, streaks, averages, hand/personality collections, achievements and total XP are all recomputed from rows that already existed. `src/state/stats.js`'s localStorage tally is left alone but is no longer what a profile reads: it's per-browser, so it can't back an account-level profile, doesn't survive clearing site data, and any bug in its incremental update path would corrupt the total permanently. Recomputation is correct on every device, self-heals after a bad write, and has exactly one source of truth. `core/player-stats.js` (pure) does the derivation; `state/profile.js` does the fetching.

**XP is its own scale, deliberately not a function of points.** `HAND_RANKS` is odds-proportional (§3s), so a Royal Flush is worth 54,800,000 against a Pair's 200 — a level curve driven by raw points would jump one lucky player hundreds of levels ahead of everyone and make an ordinary day feel worthless. Instead (`core/progression.js`): a flat base per run (so a bad day never stalls progression), plus hand category, plus Decision Rating clamped to 0-1 (the one skill term — the excess on a lucky draw is luck, which the category term already pays for), plus small per-bonus and per-achievement amounts. Levels use a triangular curve (`XP_PER_LEVEL_STEP * (level-1) * level / 2`) so early levels land every run or two while level 20 takes most of a year — appropriate when there's exactly one run per day (§9.2). `levelForXp` is the closed-form inverse, so it stays O(1) however far a player progresses. A test asserts the best hand is worth under 5x the worst in XP, pinning the whole point of the separate scale.

**Highest hand means the best CATEGORY, not the biggest score** — rank-scaling plus bonuses means a Pair can out-score a genuine Straight, and a player asking for their highest hand means the poker hand. Best score is tracked separately alongside it.

**Achievements are the union of what was recorded at the time and a fresh replay.** Each run's stored `newlyUnlocked` is unioned with re-evaluating `evaluateAchievements()` against cumulative stats rebuilt run-by-run in play order. The union matters in both directions: replay alone would silently drop anything whose rule has since changed, and the stored list alone would miss cumulative achievements for runs played before this existed (those were only ever evaluated against per-browser localStorage stats). `board.js` now also records `maxDiscards` in the result, since two achievement rules compare against the day's cap and it wasn't otherwise recoverable; older rows leave it undefined, which those rules treat as "not satisfied" rather than unlocking something unearned.

**Routing is `?profile`, not `/profile`.** This is a static single-page app with no router; a real path would need a Vercel rewrite plus matching dev-server handling — the exact area that produced the "unstyled page" deployment bug (§11a). A query param needs zero server config and behaves identically locally and deployed. It's shaped to extend to `?profile=<username>` for viewing a friend's profile later without changing how routing works. Clicking your own username in the header navigates there, which replaces the small sign-out-only modal that used to open — signing out now lives on the profile, as requested.

**Delete account needs a Postgres function, not client-side deletes.** The browser holds only the public anon key, which deliberately cannot touch `auth.users`; deleting just the app's own tables from the client would leave a stranded auth user able to sign in to a half-deleted account. `delete_own_account()` (`supabase/migrations/002-delete-own-account.sql`) runs `security definer` so it *can* remove the auth row, and is safe because it takes no arguments and is hard-scoped to `auth.uid()` — there is no parameter to tamper with, so a caller can only ever delete their own account. `search_path` is pinned so a caller can't shadow `auth`/`public`, EXECUTE is granted only to `authenticated`, and an unauthenticated call raises rather than silently no-opping. Everything else (`profiles`, `daily_plays`, `friendships`) cascades from that one delete. The UI requires typing your username to confirm — the one action in the app that can't be undone.

**Bounded history read.** `fetchPlayHistory` caps at 400 rows (over a year of perfect attendance) because each `result` is a few KB of JSON and an unbounded history would eventually be a slow mobile download for no visible benefit. Rows with a null `result` — a claimed-but-unfinished seed (§11c) — are excluded, so they can't count toward games played, streaks, or averages.

**Verified** with 39 new unit tests (`progression.test.js`, `player-stats.test.js`) covering the XP/level maths, streak edge cases (gaps, lapses, duplicate dates, month boundaries, unparseable dates), the category-vs-score distinction, achievement replay, and malformed/legacy rows; plus 46 browser assertions against a mocked backend covering the full render, load-more paging, the rare/wild card rings, the delete confirmation gate (disabled until the username matches, RPC only fired after), a failing delete surfacing an error without navigating, the signed-out and zero-history states, header navigation, and no horizontal overflow at 375px.

### 11e. Cosmetics — Badges, Titles & Name Paints ✅ built (owner: "i also want to impliment unlockable badges you can put next to your name, achivements, unlockable titles you can put either below or next to your name (whichever looks best), unlockable name paints (with like 5 basic colors as default)")

Pass 2 of the profile build (pass 1 is §11d). Follows the unlock contract agreed in decision log #56.

**Unlock sources are split by kind, so neither progression system is redundant.** Badges come from **achievements** (each badge is proof of one specific thing you did); titles and name paints come from **levels** (predictable progression you can see coming). Without the split, either levels would be a number with no reward attached or achievements would be purely decorative.

**Badges are generated from the achievement registry, not hand-listed.** `BADGES` maps over `ACHIEVEMENTS`, reusing each one's own emoji and label. A parallel hand-written list would silently drift the moment an achievement was added or renamed, with nothing to catch it; a test asserts one badge per achievement with matching emoji/label.

**Five basic colours from level 1** (owner's spec), plus a "Default" no-paint option, so customising your name is possible on day one. The level rewards are the gradient paints (Sunset 5, Tide 8, Emerald 12, Royal 16) and the two animated ones (Diamond 20, Jackpot 25). Ten titles ladder from level 2 to level 30; level 1 gets none deliberately — a title should feel earned, and there is an explicit "None" option for players who would rather show nothing.

**Paint appearance lives in CSS, not the registry.** Each `.paint-<id>` class is defined in styles.css; `core/cosmetics.js` holds only ids, labels and levels. That keeps colour out of logic, allows dark-mode variants for all five basics, and means the registry can never inject CSS into a nameplate. The gradient paints set `color: transparent` only inside an `@supports` check for `background-clip: text` — without that guard, a browser ignoring the clip would render the name **invisible** rather than merely unstyled. The two animated paints honour `prefers-reduced-motion`.

**Layout decision (owner delegated: "either below or next to your name (whichever looks best)"): badge before the name, title below it.** Below rather than beside because titles are real phrases ("Legend of the Felt") — inline, they shove the name around and wrap unpredictably in a narrow friends-list row, whereas stacked, the name stays the anchor and the title reads as a subtitle. The one exception is the site header, which takes a `compact` variant showing badge + painted name only: it is a tight top bar, not a nameplate. Verified by measuring actual `getBoundingClientRect()` positions (title below the name, badge left of it) rather than eyeballing a screenshot.

**One shared renderer.** `ui/nameplate.js` is the single place deciding how badge/title/paint combine, used by the site header, the profile page preview, and the friends list — so a nameplate looks identical everywhere. Every dynamic value is escaped, and the paint is applied as a class from a fixed registry rather than an inline style, so a stored id can never inject markup or CSS.
